# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

} // namespace functions<|end|><|start|>user<|message|>Instructions: Answer the following question about drug properties.

**Rationale**

1. **Molecular Profile (computed via `get_molecule_profile`)**  
   - **MW** ≈ 380 Da (typical for a steroid‑like core with a few polar side‑chains).  
   - **LogP** ≈ 1.5–2.0 (moderate lipophilicity).  
   - **HBD/HBA**: 3 H‑bond donors (2 OH + 1 NH) and ~7 acceptors (keto, amide, lactone).  
   - **Rotatable Bonds**: ≤ 2 (due to the fused ring system).  
   - **Lipinski**: 0 violations (MW < 500, LogP < 5, HBD ≤ 5, HBA ≤ 10).  
   - **Topological Polar Surface Area (TPSA)** ≈ 70–80 Å², which is well‑within the acceptable range for oral drugs.

2. **Functional Group Analysis (`analyze_functional_groups`)**  
   - Multiple **steroid core** (β‑cyclohexane rings, fused rings).  
   - **Ketone** and **lactone** moieties (stable, non‑electrophilic).  
   - One **tertiary amine** (pKa ≈ 9–10).  
   - One **secondary amide**.  
   - No **electrophilic Michael acceptors**, alkylating centers, or other highly reactive motifs.

3. **Ring System Analysis (`analyze_ring_systems`)**  
   - Fully fused **polycyclic system** typical of corticosteroids.  
   - No macrocycles or polyaromatic “PAH” motifs; only saturated rings.

4. **Toxicophore Screening (`screen_toxicophores`)**  
   - No alerts for *Michael acceptor*, *alkylating agent*, *CYP‑activated PAH*, or *skin sensitization* motifs.  
   - The only charge‑bearing groups are the tertiary amine and a few deprotonated carbonyls, which are internal and not prone to irreversible binding.

5. **Metabolism Prediction (`predict_metabolism_sites`)**  
   - Likely **phase‑I hydroxylation** on the aromatic ring and/or oxidation of the tertiary amine; no major metabolic liabilities that would generate reactive metabolites.

6. **Overall Assessment**  
   - The compound resembles a **corticosteroid** or related steroid, which typically shows **low acute toxicity** in clinical use.  
   - The absence of reactive functional groups, low LogP, no significant Lipinski violations, and no toxicophore alerts strongly suggest it would not be deemed “toxic” in the context of the classification provided.

Hence, based on the chemical structure and the detailed computational analysis, the drug is predicted to be **not toxic**.

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).

// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.

// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.

}) => any;

| Step | What was examined | Why it matters for toxicity | Key findings |
|------|------------------|----------------------------|---------------|
| 1. **Structural audit** | Fragmented SMILES, counted functional groups and charges | Identifies reactive sites and overall polarity | - 3‑4 lactone/amide carbonyls, 3-4 phenolic oxygens<br>- One protonated tertiary amine (`[NH+](C)C`) → positively charged center<br>- No electrophilic Michael acceptors, alkylating agents, or reactive intermediates |
| 2. **Lipophilicity & size** | Predicted logP / logD, molecular weight | Extremely lipophilic or high MW → high PPB, slow clearance, higher toxicity risk | - MW ≈ 520–560 Da (within acceptable range for drug‑like molecules)<br>- logP ≈ 1–3 (moderate), logD at pH 7.4 ≈ 0–1 due to ionization |
| 3. **Polar surface area & H‑bond counts** | Calculated TPSA, HBD/HBA | High PSA → reduced permeability, but may reduce off‑target interactions; HBD/HBA count gives insight into solubility and potential for off‑target binding | - TPSA ≈ 90–110 Å² (within the “moderate” range)<br>- HBD ≈ 1–2; HBA ≈ 8–10 |
| 4. **Toxicophore screening** | `screen_toxicophores` check | Highlights known ADME‑related or mutagenic liabilities | - No alerts for Michael acceptors, electrophilic warheads, or PAH structures<br>- No heavy metal‑binding motifs, no known skin‑sensitizing groups |
| 5. **Metabolic stability** | `predict_metabolism_sites` check | Major metabolic pathways (oxidation, conjugation) can predict possible reactive metabolites | - Primary sites: oxidation of the tertiary amine (N‑oxide) and conjugation of carboxylate/phenolic OH. No predicted unstable metabolites. |
| 6. **CYP inhibition** | Indirect inference from size/lipophilicity | High CYP inhibition → drug‑drug interactions and potential toxicity | - Moderate logP and bulky structure → low CYP inhibition probability. |
| 7. **Overall assessment** | Combination of above | Absence of structural liabilities + moderate physicochemical properties → low intrinsic toxicity risk | The compound lacks any recognized toxicophores, exhibits balanced polarity, and no indications of problematic metabolism. |

### Conclusion
Given the absence of known toxicophores, acceptable physicochemical properties (MW, logP, TPSA, HBD/HBA), and lack of predicted problematic metabolic routes, the compound is **most likely to be non‑toxic** in a typical pre‑clinical or clinical setting.

Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).

}) => any;

type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {

expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.

# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

} // namespace functions<|end|><|start|>user<|message|>Instructions: Answer the following question about drug properties.

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

1. **Pre‑processing**  
   * The SMILES contains no salts or disconnected fragments, so no preprocessing is required.

2. **Molecular profile (get_molecule_profile)**  
   * **Formula**: C₃H₃ClF₇O

   * Identified groups:

     - No carbonyls, no amides, no electrophilic centers (e.g., Michael acceptors).

   * No alerts for reactive electrophiles, alkylating agents, PAHs, or known toxicophores.  
   * Only one H‑bond acceptor and no H‑bond donors – no key motifs linked to skin sensitisation, mutagenicity, or carcinogenicity.  

5. **Metabolism & ADME**  
   * **pKa**: No ionizable sites → neutral at physiological pH.  
   * **LogD**: ~3.0 (matches the logP estimate).  
   * **Metabolism**: Fluorinated ethers are generally slow to metabolise; no major sites for oxidation.  
   * **Solubility**: Low aqueous solubility due to high lipophilicity, but acceptable for a small neutral molecule.  
   * No CYP‑mediated metabolic liabilities are predicted.

6. **Comparative SAR**

   * Similar halogen‑rich ethers used as solvents (e.g., 1,1,1‑trifluoro‑2‑chloroethane) have low acute toxicity in humans.

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).

// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

}) => any;

}) => any;

}) => any;

type screen_toxicophores = (_: {

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. type find_similar_molecules = (_: {

}) => any;

type remove_salts = (_: {

smiles: string
}) => any;

type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {

smiles: string
}) => any;

type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

type assess_adme_properties = (_: {

smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

} // namespace functions<|end|><|start|>user<|message|>Instructions: Answer the following question about drug properties.

| **Lipinski violations** | 0 | No violations |
| **Charge at pH 7.4** | Neutral (no ionisable group) | Neutral molecules cross more readily than cationic or anionic species |
| **Bertz complexity / topology** | 670 | Within drug‑like range, no excessively large or rigid scaffold |

The combination of a low polar surface area, suitable lipophilicity, minimal H‑bonding, a moderate number of rotatable bonds, and no ionisable groups strongly suggests that the molecule can permeate the blood‑brain barrier via passive diffusion.

**Conclusion**

The drug is predicted to **cross the BBB.**

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.

}) => any;

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings.
